Houses and caves are human scale, sparse, on demand. Vocabulary: **locale** (macro), **chamber** (micro), **place** (either); "room" unqualified retired. Of five band laws (§1b.3), 4 are shipped: The Blocking made law 2 (metric below the break) and law 4 (vocabulary per band) real, and law 5 waits on the first in-chamber mark | shipped (4 of 5 laws) | high | [decision 0082](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/decisions/0082-locale-chamber-place.md); [The Lintel chronicle](../chronicle/the-lintel.md); [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md) |
| CLIENT-refinement-checker | **A metric render and a topological sim stay honest by *checked refinement*** — from video codecs and compilers: the seed is canonical, the anchor graph and the solved lattice are two lowerings of it, every relation the graph asserts must be realized in the lattice, and none may be invented. **The Blocking shipped it** as eight rules, each its own test, with residual degrees of freedom reported as a number that is *exact* against what the graph leaves free — so the embedder discipline is measured, not asserted. Incidental relations must still be promoted before they gate an outcome | shipped | high | [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md); [The Blocking spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-the-blocking-design.md) |
| CLIENT-ruin-signature | **Why a place died already determines what its ruin looks like, and it is committed history rather than a generator's taste.** `OccupationRecord.cause` is `Famine`/`Burned`/`Plague`/`Fled`/`Migrated`, with `ended_by` naming the hand behind it — so soot and fallen roofs, or goods intact beside graves, or stripped fixtures, follow from the record. Baked for demography and unanticipated as the richest input the micro layer has. Wants [[SOC-casus-belli]] for the why. | raw | high | brainstorm 2026-07-27 (The Blocking), ideonomy pass 5 |
| CLIENT-district-patterns | **The settlement rung the Rose Window's territory map marks EMPTY is The Hearth's pattern machinery run one band coarser.** `Pattern { kind, attach, requires, … }` is band-agnostic, so "the-fire requires an alcove" and "the-courts require `Notability::Seat`" are the same shape: two bands, one composer, one validator. **The Blocking proved the shape one band the other way** — chamber roles admit pattern subsets, and the fire is confined to the hearthroom by the grammar rather than by a rule anyone wrote. The district rung itself is still unbuilt | shipped (the composer, one band finer) | high | [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md); [The Blocking spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-the-blocking-design.md) |
| CLIENT-depth-follows-content | **A place's depth is a function of what is there.** `MAX_DEPTH = 29` against a walk depth of 12 leaves seventeen refinements, and decision 0077 made zoom *outward* path truncation; the symmetric move is that interesting ground subdivides while wilderness stops at the walk band. A move's cost scales with its band's extent. **AMENDED 2026-07-27 (The Lintel): the automatic reading is overturned** — automatic transitions thrash, so the band changes only at a visible threshold. | raw (mechanism amended) | high | brainstorm 2026-07-27 (The Blocking), ideonomy pass 3; amended by [The Lintel chronicle](../chronicle/the-lintel.md) and [The Lintel spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-the-lintel-design.md) |
| CLIENT-brief-is-the-contract | **The committed occupation stratigraphy IS the brief the micro layer generates against.** Macro answers *who holds this land*, micro *what is standing here*, and the seam is one small derived brief — so micro is a pure function of (brief, address, seed, day) and testable without a world. Its axes are already committed in `OccupationRecord`. A **coordinate in a small orthogonal space**, never a catalogue label. Shipped as a v1 subset: the derivation reads only `built` (spec §4). | shipped (v1 subset) | high | [The Lintel spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-the-lintel-design.md) §4; [The Lintel chronicle](../chronicle/the-lintel.md) |
| CLIENT-latent-patterns | **A pattern no live read reaches is a DEFERRED epoch, not a free one.** The Blocking appended five role-gated patterns with `at_locale: false`: `selection` filters them out and `selection_for`'s only consumer commits nothing, so no metric golden moved and `room/furnishing` stayed at v1. The gate opens on the first mark committed *inside* a chamber — then all five become an epoch retroactively | shipped | high | [decision 0084](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/decisions/0084-an-epoch-is-declared-only-when-a-derivation-moved.md); [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md) |
| CLIENT-span-cap | **Technology should *bound* a room's width rather than reward it: a pre-industrial room is as wide as a roof beam can span.** Neolithic and Bronze cannot roof a wide hall; Classical can — which makes the model checkable against the tech horizons the brief already carries. Surfaced while settling the extent derivation and deliberately not adopted: a span cap and a *durable* extent are the same question about what a shell remembers, so both wait on the ruin signature the brief omits on purpose | raw | med | [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md); [The Blocking spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-the-blocking-design.md) §3.4 |
| CLIENT-durable-extent | **Derive a building's geometry from what outlives its tenants, not from who lives there now.** The Blocking derives a plan's extent from chamber count *alone*, because every richer brief signal (`notability`, `tech`, `peak_population`) describes only the **alive** occupation — so reading one would make a building shrink when its people leave. Unreachable today (nothing built stands at a ruin), which is exactly why the rule was written while it was still free. A durable extent needs the ruin signature, and arrives with it | raw | high | [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md); [The Blocking spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-the-blocking-design.md) §3.4 |
| CLIENT-content-addressed-label | **A stream label as a hash of the algorithm's source, so an epoch cannot be forgotten.** Rejected — a rename would churn world identity — but it names the residual hazard the flat per-method labels leave: a manual bump is forget-prone, and today only the artifact drift check catches a missed one. A cheaper form worth considering: pin label → output for one fixture, so changing an algorithm without bumping fails loudly rather than diffusely | rejected | med | [decision 0083](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/decisions/0083-a-label-per-algorithm-and-never-in-advance.md); [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md) |
| CLIENT-walls-bound-warmth | **A wall cell can bound warmth diffusion; a blocked edge could not.** The polarity flip behind the reification: under a boundary model a wall is purely negative (an absent adjacency), but as a *cell* it is positive — a wall is what makes an interior interior. Warmth is modelled today as pure anchor-distance decay with nothing to stop it at a boundary, so this is a real unbuilt seam between the lattice and the drive model. The Blocking only made the seam expressible | raw | high | [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md) |
| CLIENT-breach-and-rubble | **A breach is not a doorway, and the difference is a state machine on a cell.** A doorway is *designed*: it derives from a link in the anchor graph. A breach is *damage*: passable, in no link, built by nobody. The forbidden transition is the informative one — Wall → Threshold is wrong, the path is Wall → Rubble, and passable rubble is a breach. Rubble is absorbing unless someone clears it, and a structure that is all rubble is a ruin, which hands The Vestige its collapse mechanism | raw | high | [The Blocking chronicle](../chronicle/the-blocking.md) |

---

## Astronomy — the sky's near-term deficits

The astronomy domain (Campaign 2) is physically honest but **frozen and
place-less**: nothing in the sky evolves over `WorldTime` beyond calendar
phase, and there is no latitude or solar position. These rows capture the
near-term deficits — missing bodies, discarded physics, and static values that
need not be static — from a 2026-07-08 audit of the domain. SEQ-4/SEQ-5 hold
the observation-path (place-less-observer) half; these hold the
celestial-mechanics half. The **Conf** tag here reads as *how cheap and
clearly-correct the fix is*, not essay maturity — most reuse quantities the
model already computes. All point at the Campaign 2 spec's model card (§5), the
derived/approximated/drawn card each deficit extends.

| ID     | Idea                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Status | Conf | Where                                                                                     |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SKY-1  | Nothing in the sky evolves — **the moving-sky core shipped in Firm Ground II**: obliquity, eccentricity, and axial precession are now functions of `WorldTime` (`forcing.rs` `OrbitalForcing`, carrying SKY-2/4/21), and Deep Time (MAP-6) consumes them. The row's residual is now closed: **secular stellar brightening shipped in The Long Count** (`star.rs` `brightening_per_gyr`, a Sol-calibrated b = 0.10·M^2.5 per Gyr law, kin to SKY-13); semi-major-axis migration beyond the Milankovitch triad is **declined** (decision 0054) — disk-driven migration ends before a stable habitable world exists, and an isolated planet's post-genesis secular drift is effectively zero on every timescale the sim expresses. The original "MAP-6 has almost nothing to read" gap is closed | shipped | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md); [Firm Ground II spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-firm-ground-ii-design.md); [decision 0054](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/decisions/0054-no-orbital-migration.md) |
| SKY-2  | Obliquity is the *only* seasonal driver and orbits are perfectly circular (`calendar.rs` `daylight_fraction`; spec §5 declared approximation), so a zero-tilt world has *zero* seasonal variation. A single drawn **eccentricity** adds apsidal seasons — a second, tilt-independent driver giving even a zero-tilt world a year — and feeds MAP-6                                                                                                                    | shipped    | high | [Firm Ground II spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-firm-ground-ii-design.md)         |
| SKY-3  | Tidal-lock probability is a flat 5% (`anchor.rs`, `lock_roll < 0.05`), independent of orbit distance and stellar mass — yet locking is almost entirely proximity × mass. A guess wearing physics' clothes in a domain that derives; a lock timescale is computable from elements already held                                                                                                                                                                    | raw    | med  | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-4  | All bodies share epoch 0 — `moon_phase`/`year_phase`/`local_day` are all `(t/period).fract()` (`calendar.rs`), so genesis day 0 is an unintended grand alignment (every moon new, year and day beginning together). Add a drawn per-body phase offset; determinism-safe                                                                                                                                                                                          | shipped    | high | [Firm Ground II spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-firm-ground-ii-design.md)         |
| SKY-5  | Tides are computed then discarded — **shipped**: each moon commits a `moon-tide-rel` fact and raises an Ambient `tide` phenomenon (felt on both hemispheres, day or night) whose period is half its transit interval relative to the surface (local day; the year when locked), salience scaled by `tide_rel`; two-moon worlds add the **spring/neap** beat at half the moons' mutual synodic period. The SEQ-1 payoff landed measurably: 19 of the drift study's 23 locked worlds now head their pantheon with the tide (calibration `a_frozen_sky_never_heads_a_cyclic_pantheon`). Still open: the sun as a third tidal source (derivable from star mass and orbit), and a `tide` registry *concept* (deferred — concepts feed The Words' root assignment, so it rides with a language-aware change)                                                                     | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-6  | Eclipses — **shipped**: each moon draws an orbital inclination (0–10°, own stream `astronomy/moon-inclinations` after admission, so every pre-eclipse draw is byte-identical; committed as a `moon-inclination-degrees` fact) and raises an `eclipse` phenomenon where the sun is seen — total ("devours the sun whole", salience 0.90) when its disc covers SKY-7's solar disc, else a burning ring (0.75) — recurring on the node beat `synodic / ((2/π)·asin(threshold/i))`, which reproduces Earth's ~2.4 solar eclipses a year at Luna–Sol numbers. Religion took the omen unprompted: seed 42's pantheons now hold eclipse gods with derived recurrence intervals. **Refined by Eclipse Seasons**: the two gaps this row named — dated occurrences and lunar eclipses — are both shipped; see `SKY-eclipse-seasons` for the fuller build (node draw, positional ephemeris, ground tracks, the recurrence ladder) and its own row for what remains open                                                                                                                                                                                                      | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-7  | The sun has no position or angular size — **shipped**: `Calendar::solar_declination` / `solar_altitude_at` / `solar_azimuth_at` give the sun a place in the sky (the standard hour-angle formulas; retrograde worlds mirror the crossing, deepening SKY-22), `star::sun_angular_diameter_rel` gives it a disc (R = M^0.8 main-sequence relation, the sun-vs-moon comparison SKY-6 eclipses need), and the day prose tells morning from noon from evening with a twilight margin either side of night. The sky_at prose stays position-blind (fraction-through-the-arc); a situated almanac vantage reading true altitude is the natural deepening                                                                                                                                        | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-8  | Daylight length has no latitude — `daylight_fraction` returns one planet-wide number (`calendar.rs`); real day length runs from 12h-flat at the equator to 24h/0h at the poles. The astronomy twin of SEQ-5's place-less observer; blocks any latitudinally-varying climate or settlement story                                                                                                                                                                  | shipped    | high | [Firm Ground II spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-firm-ground-ii-design.md)         |
| SKY-9  | No wandering planets — the night sky is fixed stars only (`neighborhood.rs`) — **shipped**: `wanderers.rs` draws 0–4 Kepler-orbiting siblings (period from Kepler III, drawn class/albedo, an inner-planet elongation-phase brightness approximation), the `--wanderers` pin makes the count scenario-controlled, and each wanderer commits its own facts/phenomena — the morning-and-evening-star swap the almanac now narrates. Wanderer synodic calendars (SKY-wanderer-calendar) and transits (SKY-transits) remain open                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-10 | No comets or meteors — no aperiodic or long-period spectacular events, the omen-and-portent vocabulary religion (and UNI-3 trickster-noise, MAP-13 prophecy) would feed on                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | raw    | med  | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-11 | No aurorae — a latitude- and stellar-activity-gated night phenomenon (couples to SKY-8 latitude and SKY-13 activity); missing entirely                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | raw    | med  | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-12 | No constellations or asterisms — neighbors are an unstructured list (`neighborhood.rs`) — **shipped**: `figures.rs` clusters neighbors by angular separation (a 7.0° link threshold, a brightness floor of magnitude 4 admitting members, a minimum of 3 members per figure), census-calibrated (median 6 figures per system, 6.4% zero-figure skies, 66.5% with at least one figure straddling the ecliptic — the zodiac, for one boolean). A toponymy and per-species catalog (SKY-figures-per-species) remain open                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-13 | No stellar variability or flares — the star is luminosity-constant forever (`star.rs`); even within F/G/K, flare activity perturbs night and climate, and the star never *does* anything. (The 0.6–1.4 M☉ range also excludes M dwarfs — the commonest star and the natural tidal-lock generator — and A stars)                                                                                                                                                  | raw    | med  | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-14 | Sky prose collapses real structure — **shipped**: the moon-phase vocabulary now runs eight words (crescent/quarter/gibbous) on windows *centered* on the phases they name (`provider.rs` `phase_eighth` — the star-chart strip and orrery faces sample the same bands, fixing the off-center "full" window), the night sky counts every star color in brightness order instead of stamping `neighbors[0].color` on all, and season prose grades the solstice-adjacent eighths as standstills ("near their longest/shortest") between growing/shrinking                                                                     | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-15 | The ledger drops derived facts — **shipped**: `facts::genesis` now commits the star's mass/luminosity/habitable-zone bounds, the anchor's mass/orbit/insolation (one shared `insolation_rel(star, anchor)` the fact and `worldgen`'s climate input both call, so the two can no longer diverge), and each moon's mass/distance/angular-size alongside its shipped period/tide/inclination. Neighbors, the one genuinely opaque case, are promoted from a `notable-neighbor` Text blob to first-class `is-neighbor` entities carrying class/distance/brightness/declination/right-ascension (the `notable-neighbor` predicate is retired, no rename). The new `explain sky` verb (TOOL-1's fact-reading tier) reads nothing but this fact surface to narrate a world's derivation, proving the ledger self-describing                                             | shipped    | high | [The Self-Describing Sky spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-13-the-self-describing-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-16 | Binary or multiple host stars (the Tatooine case) — the host is always a single star (`star.rs` draws one mass), yet a circumbinary or close-binary world is a whole astronomy regime: two suns with their own rise/set beat, compound day–night structure, doubled shadows, and heavy cultural/religious weight. The biggest uncaptured *structural* gap — everything in SKY-1…15 assumes one sun — and a pin that reorganizes the whole sky the way `--rotation locked` does. Higher effort                                          | raw    | med  | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-17 | Sky colour from the star's spectral class — **shipped** (the prose half): daylight carries the star's light quality (K amber-and-heavy, G golden, F hard-white-edged-blue; `provider.rs` `daylight_words`, keyed on the class letter the star's name carries) and SKY-7's twilight takes the matching horizon hue. The EXP-3a scattering *regime* — colour as a computed quantity feeding perception rather than words — remains the deepening this row still points at                                                                     | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-18 | Meteor showers as *annual* phenomena — distinct from SKY-10's random meteors: a comet's debris stream produces a shower on a fixed calendar date every year, a periodic, calendar-anchored phenomenon (a different phenomenon *shape* than either the aperiodic comet or the cyclic moon). A rich religion and calendar hook                                                                                                                                                          | raw    | med  | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-19 | Marginal habitable-zone worlds — the anchor is placed *uniformly* across the √L zone (`anchor.rs`) but nothing expresses the *consequence* of sitting near an edge: a hot-edge near-desert world vs a cold-edge near-glacial one. The orbit is drawn, its climatic meaning discarded. Kin to SKY-15's missing insolation fact, but the point here is the *edge extremes* climate would read. Update (The Self-Describing Sky, 2026-07-13): edge-of-zone is now answerable from the ledger alone — `hab-zone-inner-au`/`hab-zone-outer-au` and `insolation-rel` are committed facts — but the *consequence* (a hot-edge near-desert vs. cold-edge near-glacial world) still isn't expressed anywhere; this row's remaining gap is unchanged                                                                                          | raw    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-20 | Moon phase conflates sidereal and synodic periods (**confirmed defect**) — `moon.period` is a Kepler *sidereal* period (base `27.32` in `moons.rs`), but illumination phase (new/full) cycles with the *synodic* period, ~29.53 d for an Earth-like moon; `calendar.rs` `moon_phase` and `months_per_year` feed the sidereal period straight in, an ~8% error (the almanac's own fixture prose quotes the synodic "29.5 days / 12.4 months" while the code derives ~13.4). A physics bug, not a modelling choice — distinct from SKY-14's vocabulary; fix is the known synodic formula | shipped | high | [Star Chart spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-star-chart-design.md)         |
| SKY-21 | Moon-stabilized obliquity — a large moon damps a world's axial wobble (Earth); without one, obliquity wanders chaotically (Mars). A derivable coupling between moon presence (the SKY-5 machinery) and the obliquity-drift half of SKY-1, turning one draw into two coupled consequences                                                                                                                                                                                             | shipped    | med  | [Firm Ground II spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-firm-ground-ii-design.md)         |
| SKY-22 | Retrograde rotation — **shipped**: `Rotation::Spinning` carries a `retrograde` bit, drawn rare (authored prior 10%, own stream `astronomy/spin-direction` so nothing else moves) or pinned (`--spin prograde\|retrograde`, conflicting loudly with a locked rotation), committed as a `retrograde-spin` flag fact when true, and told in the day sky ("Here it rises in the west and sets in the east"). Direction never changes the day's length or timing; the orrery's drawn spin direction and any azimuthal machinery (SKY-7) would be the consumers that deepen it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-23 | Astronomy's verification surface is thinner than its billing — **shipped** (the cross-tier half): `domains/astronomy/tests/tier_refinement.rs` pins "coarse constrains fine" as a four-test battery over 32 seeds × 3 regimes × several hours — every generated sky keeps tier 0's whole claim (exactly one top-salience day-sky sun, nothing rivaling it, "the sun" never retracted from the visible bodies) and may only add structure beneath it (the sun's added period must be the calendar's own day; a locked sun stays aperiodic like tier 0's). **The star/anchor/neighbor batteries shipped in The Long Count** (`domains/astronomy/tests/genesis_properties.rs`): four batteries over 256 seeds — star mass bounds and monotone derivations, anchor orbit/Kepler/rotation invariants, neighbor counts/coordinates/byte-identical regeneration, and the alignment-drift dating round-trip — closing the broader appetite on the same scale moons already enjoyed | shipped    | high | [Campaign 2 spec §5](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-05-campaign-2-the-sky-design.md)         |
| SKY-24 | Insolation is rotation-regime-blind — **fixed** (The Terminator). `annual_mean_insolation` computed habitability from latitude alone, never branching on regime, so on a locked world substellar, antistellar and terminator read identical insolation — the field could not see the terminator. Temperature already branched on `Locked`. Fixed by a shared `substellar_cosine` helper, spinning worlds byte-identical. **The religion payoff it was chased for did NOT follow:** presiding-belief selection is independent of the physics — a separate blindness, [[SKY-25]]. Determinism-relevant | shipped | high | [The Terminator spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-16-the-terminator-design.md); [The Terminator chronicle](../chronicle/the-terminator.md) |
| SKY-25 | Presiding-belief selection is dominance-blind — found by [[SKY-24]]'s acceptance battery. The first-minted belief is gated by the registry's alphabetical order plus the founder-floor guarantee, not by dominance. **Corrected by The Named:** that species is goblin, not bugbear — bugbear sorts first but places on 0 of 1000 seeds. *Registry-first is not placed-first.* **Resolved by The Presiding — DISSOLVED, not repaired.** Dominance-awareness moves the tide payoff 0/9 → 0/9: hobgoblin's own pantheon also heads Eternal. The world-level metric was retired: a world has no religion, its peoples do | shipped | high | [The Presiding spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-the-presiding-design.md); [The Presiding chronicle](../chronicle/the-presiding.md) |

### The enrichment atlas — substitutions beyond the deficit audit

A 2026-07-10 ideonomy pass over the domain — substitution along five
dimensions (hierarchy, homogeneity, distribution, visibility, polarity) —
generating enrichment directions rather than deficit fixes. IDs are
category+slug per decision
[`0026-slugs-not-numbers`](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/decisions/0026-slugs-not-numbers.md).
**Conf** reads as in the deficit table: *how cheap and clearly-correct the
work is*. The visibility-dimension rows (seasonal-night-sky,
heliacal-risings, circumpolar, stale-alignments) are pure consumers of
data already drawn and currently decorative — the cheapest real
enrichment in the domain.

| ID | Idea | Status | Conf | Where |
| -- | ---- | ------ | ---- | ----- |
| SKY-moon-world | The anchor as a satellite — demote the habitable world to a moon of a gas giant riding the habitable zone: the primary dominates the sky at tens of lunar diameters, the “month” becomes one orbit of the giant, an eclipse-by-primary recurs every orbit (at a fixed local time in the locked case), and the giant’s phases light the night. SKY-16 restructures the top of the sky’s tree; this restructures the middle — a pin-grade regime | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-rings | A debris annulus around the anchor — a permanent sky arc whose apparent shape is a pure function of latitude (an edge-on wire at the equator, a high arch from temperate zones), a ring shadow darkening the winter hemisphere (a real, derivable climate driver), and a natural origin in a moon carried inside the Roche limit — coupling to machinery `moons.rs` already has | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-resonances | Resonance architecture among moons — today they are drawn independently, related only by a stability cap: Laplace-style period chains, near-commensurabilities yielding rare grand-conjunction super-cycles (a computable decades-scale omen), mutual occultations, and the 1:1 case — a co-orbital Trojan companion hanging forever 60° from the sun | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-galactic-situation | Draw the stellar-density regime and make the 2–5 neighbor count its consequence — a cluster world with hundreds of brilliant neighbors and no true dark (the Nightfall case) vs a void world with an empty black sky; one draw (and pin) that rewrites navigation, religion, and the value of night wholesale | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-moon-individuation | Moons come from one recipe and differ only in mass and distance; give them kinds — captured vs co-formed origin, retrograde orbits (a moon crossing the sky backward), albedo and color, and, a close moon being itself tidally locked, a fixed face with drawn markings: the “face in the moon” as durable, nameable identity for myth and lexicon (LANG hooks). **The Faces (hornvale#4) shipped the presentation-side slice; The Reckoning shipped the origin-and-retrograde half of the sim-side ask:** `scene/moons/v1` gives each moon albedo/color/cratering/maria as seeded hash-noise render descriptors (a visible face), and astronomy now draws each moon a formation mechanism (`GiantImpact`/`Capture`) driving coeval-vs-decoupled age, iron-poor-vs-alien density, and regular-vs-irregular inclination (retrograde past 90°) — committed as ledger facts (`moon-formation`, `moon-age-gyr`, `moon-density`) a myth or lexicon consumer can name, not merely a render-lens cosmetic. What remains open: albedo/color/markings are still drawn by the render lens rather than tied to the origin story, and the parked Moons campaign (campaign B, unblocked by The Reckoning's real density) still owns the better crater model (highlands saturate, maria reset the clock) the "face in the moon" imagery ultimately wants | shipped | high | this conversation; ideonomy session; [The Faces](../chronicle/the-faces.md); [The Reckoning](../chronicle/the-reckoning.md) |
| SKY-rotation-regimes | Spinning/locked are two values of a richer dimension — a 3:2 spin-orbit resonance gives a solar day two years long (double sunrise at perihelion for some longitudes); near-lock with libration gives a sun that nods at the horizon along the terminator; eccentricity gates which regime a close-in world settles into. Widens SKY-3’s lock physics into a regime enum; SKY-22’s retrograde bit is one flag of it | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-extreme-obliquity | Above ~54° tilt the annual insolation gradient inverts — the poles receive more than the equator — and a sideways Uranus-like world alternates hemispheric day and night by season; make the regime reachable in the draw, pinnable, and read by climate rather than assumed away | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-calendar-hardness | How commensurate a world’s periods are (synodic months per year, days per year near an integer or maximally awkward) is exactly the intercalation difficulty MAP-18’s cultures will one day face: measure its distribution across the seed census as a Lab metric (the decision-0011 posture) — ground-truth hardness to score cultural calendars against later | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-diffuse-sky | Night light is a finite list of points; add the extended components — the galactic band, zodiacal light, integrated starfield glow — as a brightness field over (direction, time): moonlight then suppresses it (a full moon erases the band — one phenomenon interacting with another) and dark-sky quality becomes a computable, place- and phase-dependent quantity | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-eclipse-seasons | Eclipses are a rate, not an event — **shipped**: one new draw (each moon's ascending-node longitude, stream `moon-nodes`) feeds a derived first-order nodal regression, a positional ecliptic moon ephemeris, and closed-form syzygy enumeration (`eclipses.rs`), turning SKY-6's mean recurrence into dated solar *and* lunar events clustered into real eclipse seasons. Ships shadow ground tracks (a latitude band swept across longitude, with sight tiers — whole-sun, burning-ring, bitten, unseen — partitioning the globe honestly) and the full recurrence ladder: draconic month, eclipse year, a closed-form best-cycle search (recovers the true 223/242 saros from true Luna inputs; a world's own derived node period may legitimately land elsewhere), the exeligmos, series lifetime, and the parade rate. The coarse SKY-6 rate phenomenon stays byte-identical as the untouched coarse tier, bound to the new dated tier by a constitutional consistency guard. Deliberately out of scope: standstills (the 18.6-year moonrise-azimuth extreme) — this campaign ships its hard prerequisite only; see `SKY-standstills` | shipped | high | [Eclipse Seasons chronicle entry](../chronicle/eclipse-seasons.md) |
| SKY-standstills | The 18.6-year moonrise-azimuth extreme (the lunar standstill) — deliberately deferred by Eclipse Seasons, whose hard prerequisite it names as shipped: a positional moon ephemeris (`moon_ecliptic_longitude_deg`/`moon_ecliptic_latitude_deg`), the node machinery (`node_longitude_at`, `node_regression_period`), and the committed `moon-node-period-days` fact per moon are all in place. What's unbuilt is the instrument itself — moonrise azimuth as a function of declination and latitude, its major/minor standstill envelope, and the consumer story SKY-stale-alignments' ground half leaves open: monument alignments cut to a standstill extreme rather than a solstice, and their own dating-by-drift reading | raw | high | [Eclipse Seasons chronicle](../chronicle/eclipse-seasons.md) |
| SKY-guest-stars | The once-ever event — a nova or supernova drawn over deep time completes the event-shape taxonomy (periodic moons, calendar-anchored showers SKY-18, aperiodic-recurring comets SKY-10, once-ever transient) and gives chronology its absolute anchor: independent cultural chronicles synchronize on “the year of the new star” (MAP-8/MEM hooks) | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-proper-motion | Give neighbors drawn space velocities — constellations deform over deep time, close passes brighten then fade (the brilliant “elder star” of the old songs now dim, with almanac ground truth), making myth drift scoreable: MAP-4’s celestial sibling. Distinct from SKY-1, which is the anchor’s own elements, not the neighborhood’s | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-seasonal-night-sky | The sun occupies a place among the fixed stars, so which neighbors are visible at night is a function of year phase — winter stars and summer stars — **shipped**: `night_sky_at` (night_sky.rs) is the unified derived view — visible/circumpolar/never-rises stars computed from each neighbor's precession-drifted equatorial position against the observer's latitude and the sun's position at transit, recomputed on demand, committed nowhere | shipped | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-heliacal-risings | The first pre-dawn reappearance of a bright neighbor after its conjunction with the sun is the sharpest annual event naked-eye astronomy owns (the Sothic move) — **shipped**: `heliacal_events` (heliacal.rs) scans a year at 400 samples for the rising/setting edges of each star's dawn-and-dusk visibility against a class-graded arcus-visionis threshold (7–11°, brighter classes cutting through brighter twilight), yielding the rising/setting pair and the absence fraction between them; the stellar calendar family it founds (SKY-wanderer-calendar's sibling) is not yet consumed | shipped | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-circumpolar | At latitude φ stars with \|dec\| > 90°−φ never set and their mirror set never rises: hemispheres see genuinely different skies — **shipped**: `night_sky_at` partitions every neighbor into circumpolar/never-rises/visible by the sign-and-extremity test on precession-drifted declination, and a system-level (latitude-independent) pole-star search within `POLE_STAR_MAX_SEPARATION_DEG` (10°) retires and crowns pole stars as precession moves the pole — committed as facts and read by the almanac | shipped | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-stale-alignments | `forcing.rs` computes `precession_at` and nothing reads it: precession drifts the pole and the solstice-rise azimuths over kiloyears, so a monument aligned at its founding goes measurably stale — dating-by-alignment, the sky as archaeological clock. The sky half **shipped** (night-sky campaign: `precession_at` finally has readers — star positions, pole-star retirement, heliacal drift); **the ground half shipped in The Long Count**: `Calendar::alignment_drift_deg` (solstice-sunrise azimuth drift, cos az = sin ε(t)/cos φ) and its dating inverse, the `alignments` worldgen stage committing per-settlement `founding-solstice-azimuth-degrees` facts at genesis, and the almanac surfacing both the founding sightline and the slow drift beneath it. Both halves of the row are now closed | shipped | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-wanderer-calendar | The fourth naked-eye calendar family — wanderer synodic periods (Venus-calendar territory: morning/evening-star cycles, retrograde loops, heliacal risings of the wanderers themselves) — enabled by the shipped wanderers (SKY-9), but nothing derives it yet | raw | high | night-sky-instrument campaign close |
| SKY-transits | Wanderer transits and occultations — a composite consumer crossing the shipped wanderers against the solar disc (SKY-7) and the moons (SKY-5/6): a wanderer's silhouette crossing the sun, or a moon occulting a wanderer, neither derived today. **Prerequisite shipped by Eclipse Seasons**: the positional moon ephemeris (`moon_ecliptic_longitude_deg`, `moon_ecliptic_latitude_deg`) this row needs to place a moon's disc against a wanderer's now exists; the crossing derivation itself is still unbuilt | raw | med | night-sky-instrument campaign close; [Eclipse Seasons chronicle](../chronicle/eclipse-seasons.md) |
| SKY-figures-per-species | The figure-clustering brightness floor (SKY-12) is a declared reference-observer convention, not a universal one — cross with EXP-3 perception to derive per-species figure catalogs, where a keener or dimmer-sighted people sees a genuinely different sky's worth of shapes | raw | high | night-sky-instrument campaign close |
| SKY-variable-stars | Periodic brightness oscillation — position oscillates everywhere in the shipped sky (moon phase, wanderer elongation, precession drift) but brightness itself never does; a variable-star draw would give the derived starfield (SKY-12's substrate) its own pulse | raw | med | night-sky-instrument campaign close |
| SKY-impacts | Everything the sky does today is benign spectacle; impacts add the negative pole — crater scars committed as deep-time terrain fossils, an impact winter as a climate event — gated rare with a derived ignition and a bounded footprint (the UNI-15 discipline) so it stays omen-grade, never apocalypse-grade | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-tidal-braking | Tides brake the spin, so days lengthen over deep time (the year once held 400 days — and fossil day-counts are checkable ground truth, the coral-ring move) while the moon spirals outward, so total eclipses thin to annular and eventually die; the melancholy secular arc the shipped Milankovitch triad leaves out, reusing `tide_rel`, which SKY-5 already wants surfaced. **Insertion point named by Eclipse Seasons**: the model card's evaluate-at-t rule (angular sizes and rates are functions of the event's own time, never cached, in `sun_angular_rel_at` and every dated-eclipse threshold) is the seam a secular moon-recession law can slide under without an epoch — still unbuilt | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session; [Eclipse Seasons chronicle](../chronicle/eclipse-seasons.md) |
| SKY-equation-of-time | With eccentricity and obliquity both live, true noon and mean noon diverge by a computable annual curve (the analemma — a per-world fingerprint): the first benign, subtle falsehood a measuring culture can discover, the sundial that lies — UNI-1’s cheapest anomaly | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-authored-sky | A third provider whose sky is deliberately non-physical but internally coherent (dome and chariot; Ptolemaic clockwork) — consumers must never learn which system produced a phenomenon, and an authored sky is the strongest possible test of that promise; comparing the religions grown under a true sky vs an authored one is UNI-2’s controlled-science move applied to astronomy itself | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| SKY-neighbor-names | The sim names no star: neighbors are anonymous ledger entities whose display identity is color + class ("the amber giant"). Give each culture proper names for its bright neighbors — drawn through the language domain's own phonology like deity names, committed as per-species facts, so the almanac and `explain sky` can say who *this* people call the winter star; the natural companion of SKY-figures-per-species (figure toponymy) and a prerequisite for myth that cites a star by name | raw | med | [The Real Sky spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-the-real-sky-design.md) §7 |

### Exotic system architectures — the cells the model does not occupy

A 2026-07-16 sweep of **real, observed** system architectures against the
shape of astronomy's draw. The finding is uncomfortable, and worth stating
once rather than thirteen times: **reality is stranger than the model**.
Hornvale generates exactly one cell of the configuration space — one star,
one terrestrial anchor, N moons — while nearly every row below is a
configuration the solar system or the Kepler catalogue *already ships*.
(The Reckoning, later the same day, moved the moon clause off its own
deficit list: a moon now draws a formation mechanism, so N is no longer
uniformly small-regular-prograde — a `Capture` moon can be alien in
density, decoupled in age, and irregular enough to be retrograde. The
single-star, single-anchor cell is the deficit that remains.) "Strange new
worlds" is therefore not a licence to fabricate; it is a mandate to catch
up, and the rows below are stated as deficits against observed fact, not as
invention.

Each row names **what it costs the model**, which is the part a future
reader actually needs. The tier boundary is the load-bearing distinction:

- **Additive** — new draws and streams behind existing shapes, no spine
  change; pin-grade work of the kind SKY-22 already proved.
- **Spine-breaking** — attacks `Anchor` being singular and `sky_of(world)`
  assuming one world with one sky. These are a **program**, not a campaign,
  and must never be costed as one.

Six of the thirteen configurations swept were **already banked** and are
deliberately not re-filed: rings and their Roche-limit origin
(`SKY-rings`), the circumbinary/Tatooine regime (SKY-16), the habitable
moon (`SKY-moon-world`), extreme obliquity (`SKY-extreme-obliquity`), and
retrograde rotation (SKY-22 — not merely banked but *shipped*). **Conf**
reads as in the atlas above: *how cheap and clearly-correct the work is* —
never how real the phenomenon is, since the physics of every row below is
observed.

| ID | Idea | Status | Conf | Where |
| -- | ---- | ------ | ---- | ----- |
| SKY-binary-planet | Two comparable-mass worlds about a barycenter **outside both bodies** — real: Pluto–Charon, which makes this configuration not exotic but simply absent. Mutually tidally locked, so each hangs *permanently motionless* in the other's sky, a disc close enough to show weather crossing it: a second inhabited surface you can watch a storm move over, and never once see rise. **Spine-breaking, and the sharpest case of it** — `Anchor` is singular by construction and `sky_of(world)` assumes one world with one sky, so this needs two anchors, a shared barycentric orbit, and a sky that knows which surface it is seen from. The tide, calendar, and eclipse machinery (SKY-5, SKY-6) all become two-ended. A program, not a campaign | raw | low | [frontier §map](frontier.md#the-frontier-map) |
| SKY-trojan-world | A second world sharing the anchor's orbit exactly, librating about L4/L5 sixty degrees ahead or behind — **observed at moon scale**: Tethys keeps both Telesto and Calypso. A sibling world at *identical* insolation and period that never approaches and never recedes. `SKY-resonances` already banks the 1:1 case as a *companion body* hanging sixty degrees from the sun; this row is its escalation — the co-orbital is a **world**, which is what makes it **spine-breaking** rather than one more wanderer draw (SKY-9). Two anchors again, but unlike `SKY-binary-planet` the two skies are near-identical and the two climates are the same climate, which makes this the cheapest honest probe of a two-anchor model | raw | low | [frontier §map](frontier.md#the-frontier-map) |
| SKY-horseshoe-pair | Co-orbitals that periodically **exchange orbits** — real, and not rare: Janus and Epimetheus trade every four years, the inner one catching the outer, swapping angular momentum, and becoming it. A neighbour world that closes for years, swaps places with you, then recedes for years: a clock nobody designed and no culture would have chosen, which is precisely the intercalation nightmare `SKY-calendar-hardness` exists to score. Additive if the co-orbital is a body (a wanderer whose distance beats on a decades-scale cycle); spine-breaking if it is a second anchor, as in `SKY-trojan-world`. **Names a real tension with the Lorenz guard-rail**: co-orbital horseshoe dynamics are chaotic on long horizons, so this can never be a forward integrator seeded from quantized ledger floats — it needs either a closed-form libration evaluated at t (the model card's evaluate-at-t rule) or a full-precision checkpoint format of its own | raw | low | [frontier §map](frontier.md#the-frontier-map) |
| SKY-quasi-satellite | A body in *stellar* orbit that nonetheless **appears to circle the world**, for millennia, without ever being gravitationally bound to it — real: 3753 Cruithne is Earth's. A "moon" that is not a moon: present in the sky, absent from the gravity model, raising no tide (SKY-5) and obeying no Kepler period about the anchor. Additive and cheap, being a sky-appearance draw rather than a dynamics change — and it is the sharpest *epistemic* body the domain could hold: a measuring culture that assumes everything circling it is bound derives a mass that does not exist. UNI-1's anomaly move (kin to `SKY-equation-of-time`'s lying sundial) handed a body instead of a curve. **Filed by The Reckoning** as the sibling deficit to its shipped capture draw: that campaign gave the model a body whose *origin* is alien; this one banks a body whose *bondage* is illusory | raw | med | [frontier §map](frontier.md#the-frontier-map); [The Reckoning](../chronicle/the-reckoning.md) |
| SKY-submoon | A moon of a moon — the one row here with **no observed instance**, though the dynamics are unobjectionable inside a narrow stability window (a large, distant primary moon holding a small, close submoon; the window is real but tight, which is the likeliest reason none has been found). Adds a **level to the hierarchy**: `moons.rs` draws a flat list keyed to the anchor, so a submoon needs the satellite tree to nest — the same shape change `SKY-moon-world` makes at the top of the tree, made at the bottom. The payoff is a sky where one moon visibly holds another, and phases within phases; the risk is that an honest stability window almost never yields one, making it a draw that rarely fires | raw | med | [frontier §map](frontier.md#the-frontier-map) |
| SKY-minimoon | Temporary capture — a body held for a few orbits and then lost; real and recurrent, with 2006 RH120 and 2020 CD3 each Earth's for roughly a year. **A moon with an expiry date**: the only body in the domain whose existence is a function of *when you look* — a satellite your grandchildren will not have and your grandparents did not. Additive (a drawn capture epoch and duration over the shipped moon machinery) but it breaks one quiet assumption worth naming: the moon roster is a genesis-time constant today, not a function of `WorldTime`. Kin to `SKY-guest-stars` in event-shape (the transient that happens once) but satellite-grade, and the natural companion to `SKY-moon-individuation`'s captured-origin draw — which banks capture as a *permanent* origin story, never as a thing that un-happens. **Filed by The Reckoning, whose permanent capture has now shipped**, which sharpens this row from a companion idea into a named deficit: astronomy draws `Capture` as a genesis-time outcome, so the assumption this row breaks — that the moon roster is a constant, not a function of `WorldTime` — is now load-bearing in merged code rather than merely implicit | raw | med | [frontier §map](frontier.md#the-frontier-map); [The Reckoning](../chronicle/the-reckoning.md) |
| SKY-s-type-binary | The world orbits **one** star of a binary while the companion hangs in the sky as an intensely bright non-sun — real, and the nearest system to us: the α Centauri configuration. Distinct from SKY-16, whose picture is the circumbinary/Tatooine regime (two suns, doubled shadows, a compound day, the host itself binary). Here the host stays single and the companion is a *second light source*, not a second primary: bright enough to cast a shadow and to kill true night for part of the year, never bright enough to be a sun. That makes it **additive where SKY-16 is spine-breaking** — a drawn companion mass and separation feeding brightness and a long orbital beat, with no second host and no compound day. The cheapest real entry into binarity, and the row that most changes the value of night (`SKY-diffuse-sky`, `SKY-galactic-situation`) | raw | med | [frontier §map](frontier.md#the-frontier-map) |
| SKY-moonlight-occlusion | The **third** occluder. Daylight hides the stars (the venue split) and cloud now dims them (The Occlusion); a full moon washing out the faint neighbour stars is the same mechanism a third time — and the only one astronomy could own *entirely alone*, with no weather knowledge at all, since it already holds every moon's phase and angular diameter. Feeds the shipped `Visibility` seam, so the machinery exists; what is missing is the brightness model that turns phase plus size into a sky-glow ratio. The row that makes a full-moon night feel different from a new-moon one | raw | high (small) | [The Occlusion](../chronicle/the-occlusion.md) |

---

## The climate domain — enrichment directions

Twenty-one enrichments of the *shipped* climate crate (`domains/climate`:
tier-1 `GeneratedClimate` derives temperature, moisture, a 22-class biome
field, and a binary habitability mask over the shared Geosphere), generated by
a 2026-07-10 ideonomy pass over its current shape. IDs are category+slug per
decision [`0026-slugs-not-numbers`](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/decisions/0026-slugs-not-numbers.md).
**Conf** reads as in the SKY atlas: *how cheap and clearly-correct the work
is*. The abstraction-lift framing that organizes them: the model is a field of
potential (insolation) redistributed by transport operators (winds, currents)
against radiative sinks and moisture sources, bent by feedback loops — and
today it has exactly one transport channel (zonal wind, used once for the rain
shadow) and **zero closed feedback loops**, every field a single analytic
pass. The three load-bearing moves: `CLIM-operators` (a relaxation/operator
spine) is the enabler the feedback rows all need; `CLIM-ocean-currents` is the
highest-fidelity single addition (the model card's headline omission); and
`CLIM-paleoclimate-coupling` is the highest-leverage for the wider sim,
turning climate from a day-0 snapshot into history's first substrate on
machinery MAP-6 already shipped.

| ID | Idea | Status | Conf | Where |
| -- | ---- | ------ | ---- | ----- |
| CLIM-operators | The enabling refactor — recast `generate()` from a one-shot monolith into named, individually-testable operators (insolation → advection → precipitation → feedback relaxation) that compose, so every feedback row below lands as a module without editing the others: the "adding a domain never edits an existing one" constitutional rule taken one level down, into the crate. Do it first, or the other twenty fight the monolith. **Re-timed (The Gyre, 2026-07-19):** its trigger is **cyclicity** (a feedback loop needing relaxation), not the number of fields — an ideonomy pass established that vector fields alone (The Gyre) earn none of it, correcting The Turning §7's plan to pull it into the winds campaign. It moves to the **level-2 feedback campaign** (`CLIM-ocean-currents` SST advection / `CLIM-ice-albedo`), where the first loop demands the solver spine. | raw | high | this conversation; re-timed by The Gyre |
| CLIM-ocean-currents | The model card's headline omission — a wind-driven surface-current field (gyres from the band winds × a Coriolis sign × coastline deflection) advecting the SST field, warming poleward-western coasts and cooling equatorward-eastern ones, moderating continentality from the sea. The single highest-fidelity addition; feeds marine biomes and coastal land temperature. **The vector field shipped (The Gyre, 2026-07-19, Weather Program C2)** — level 0: an Ekman-deflected, coast-projected kinematic current field emitted as `scene/tiles/v1` `current_east`/`current_north`, advected on the Living Globe; byte-identical (no temperature coupling). The **SST-advection feedback** (the coastal-moderation payoff — it moves the census-read mean → an epoch) is deliberately deferred to the level-2 feedback campaign, which carries `CLIM-operators`. | raw | med | [The Gyre spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-ocean-currents-design.md) (vector field shipped); SST feedback raw |
| CLIM-ice-albedo | Close the first feedback loop — today temperature → ice is one-way; let ice and snow raise albedo, lowering absorbed insolation, and relax to a fixed point. Yields snowball/temperate bistability, sharp ice margins, and hysteresis under paleoclimate forcing (CLIM-paleoclimate-coupling); needs the CLIM-operators solver | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-greenhouse | A world-level greenhouse scalar (outgassed in deep-time terrain, or pinnable) shifting the whole temperature baseline — the missing knob that finally lets the drawn-then-discarded insolation (SKY-15) and habitable-zone edge (SKY-19) express a climatic consequence: a cold-edge world saved by a thick atmosphere, a hot-edge runaway. Cheap, and its consumers are already waiting | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-clouds | The other missing feedback — a diagnostic cloud-fraction field from moisture × uplift that dims insolation in the wet rising belts (cooling) and traps longwave at night (warming), breaking the model's perfect insolation→temperature transparency even before it is made iterative. **Diagnostic half shipped** (The Rains, Weather Program C3, 2026-07-19): the cloud-fraction field (moisture × uplift) is emitted and the client shades and advects it, but it is strictly diagnostic — the insolation/longwave feedback is a loop and stays deferred to the level-2 feedback campaign (cyclicity, not field count, is that solver's trigger). **Typed cloud presentation shipped** (The Firmament C4 emitted per-tile cloud TYPE; The Mantle, 2026-07-19, renders it): the globe wears a cloud-texture shell — fbm noise gated by cloud type into a transparent equirectangular texture on a sphere above the surface, occluded far-side, gentle drift. The insolation feedback remains the level-2 deferral | shipped (diagnostic + typed render) | med | [The Rains spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-precipitation-design.md); [The Mantle chronicle](../chronicle/the-mantle.md); [cloud-shell spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-cloud-shell-design.md) |
| CLIM-moisture-transport | Replace the single-pass rain shadow (`moisture.rs`) with advected precipitable water — an ocean evaporation source, transport along the wind field, orographic and convective sinks — so continental interiors dry with distance-from-sea and successive ranges compound their shadows: the honest version of the declared single-pass approximation. **Shipped** (The Rains, Weather Program C3, 2026-07-19): a bounded upwind budget trace — an evaporation source over the sea against orographic, convective, and distance-decay sinks — modulating a preserved base-wetness floor (spinning worlds only; locked worlds keep the substellar model). The moisture field the biomes read is now transport-derived, a derived-field epoch (biomes → settlement/species/culture/language cascade) | shipped | med | [The Rains spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-precipitation-design.md); [The Rains chronicle](../chronicle/the-rains.md) |
| CLIM-precip-units | Promote dimensionless moisture `[0,1]` to real precipitation (mm/yr, a validating newtype per the typed-quantities rule) split into rain vs snow by temperature, with a seasonal distribution regime (uniform / summer-max / winter-max / monsoon). The notation substrate CLIM-cryosphere, CLIM-koppen, and CLIM-monsoon all read. **Shipped** (The Rains, Weather Program C3, 2026-07-19): a validating `Precipitation` newtype (mm/yr, Earth-ranged), a temperature-driven snow fraction, and a categorical seasonal regime, all additive on the transport-derived moisture; biomes keep classifying on the dimensionless `[0,1]`, so no classifier rewrite | shipped | high | [The Rains spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-precipitation-design.md); [The Rains chronicle](../chronicle/the-rains.md) |
| CLIM-precip-phase | Hydrometeor phase — sleet, freezing rain, ice pellets, hail — classified from the vertical temperature profile (a melting layer aloft over a subfreezing surface). Sleet/freezing-rain a level-0 diagnostic over a 2-layer temp column (a later refinement of rain/snow); hail is convective → the drawn-storms campaign | raw | med | [The Rains spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-precipitation-design.md) §7 |
| CLIM-cryosphere | Snowpack, glaciers, and permafrost as slow water stores — an accumulation-minus-ablation balance yielding glacier extent (a deep-time terrain erosion driver) and permafrost depth (a habitability gate and a latent methane feedback). Couples climate → terrain → deep time; needs typed precipitation (CLIM-precip-units) | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-endorheic | Closed-basin hydrology — route precipitation as runoff over the elevation field so interior drainage forms salt playas (arid) or great lakes (wet), which then moderate their own local climate (lake-effect snow, raised humidity): the climate↔terrain feedback the flat per-cell field forecloses | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-monsoon | A seasonal wind reversal where large continents heat and cool faster than adjacent oceans, pulling seasonal rains inland — emergent from the continentality and seasonal-amplitude terms `temperature.rs` already computes, so a named regime rather than new physics. The strongest cheap seasonality win | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-diurnal | The day/night temperature range as a function of continentality × humidity × cloud (huge in deserts, tiny over oceans), coupling to the shipped latitudinal day length (SKY-8): the analytic fast layer, distinct from stochastic weather (CLIM-weather) | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-koppen | A named macro-classification (tropical / arid / temperate / continental / polar and subtypes) derived from temperature and precipitation *seasonality*, sitting above biomes as a coarser, human-legible zone vocabulary for the almanac and settlement layers — a near-pure consumer of fields already derived, the cheapest real enrichment here (the SKY visibility-row analog) | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-oscillations | A deterministic quasi-periodic ocean–atmosphere index (an ENSO/NAO analog) driving multi-year drought and flood pulses; seeded, so replayable and drift-safe. The interannual rate between weather and paleoclimate, and the natural forcing for famine (BIO-8) and migration | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-paleoclimate-coupling | Wire the shipped Milankovitch cycling (MAP-6) into the temperature baseline and ice-albedo so biomes *migrate* across deep time — turning the day-0-frozen field into a time-parameterized one, history's first substrate. Highest-leverage for the wider sim; the machinery exists, so this is coupling work, not new physics | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-weather | The genuinely missing layer the observation path (SEQ-4) wants — a seeded, deterministic-from-seed weather generator (fronts, storms, heat waves, cold snaps) sampled at a place and day for the REPL and phenomena stream: the fast stochastic texture over the smooth analytic climate, the "models author, dice roll" pattern applied to weather. **Observation half shipped** (The Firmament, Weather Program C4, 2026-07-19): a sampled synoptic weather-state field (clear/fair/overcast/rain/storm + cirrus) from a per-cell storm propensity modulated by a time-smooth drifting-Fbm phase — sampled, never stepped (the Lorenz guard-rail by construction), felt at a place and day in possession and the almanac. Level 0, byte-identical (writes no fact). Deferred sequels: the storminess-climatology→religion coupling that grows **weather-gods** (an epoch), storm-as-object cyclone tracks, and the globe's typed-cloud *rendering* (the sim ships per-tile cloud type; the orrery consumer + a legible cloud-rendering approach wait on a follow-up) | shipped (observation) | med | [The Firmament spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-weather-design.md); [The Firmament chronicle](../chronicle/the-firmament.md) |
| CLIM-hazards | The negative pole — drought, flood, wildfire (a fire-return interval from aridity × seasonality × fuel), tropical cyclones (warm SST × a latitude window), blizzards, dust storms — committed as rare phenomena/facts under the omen-grade discipline (UNI-15), feeding history and the disturbance-climax biomes (fire-maintained savanna, flood-plain forest) the equilibrium Whittaker lookup cannot produce. Sibling of SKY-impacts and DOM-15 geohazards | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-graded-habitability | Flip `habitability.rs`'s binary mask into a continuous `[0,1]` suitability surface with named stressors (heat, cold, aridity, seasonal variance), feeding settlement placement and carrying capacity (MAP-7) with texture instead of a cliff edge. Cheap and clearly correct | raw | high | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-desertification | The dark feedback of the water cycle — vegetation loss (BIO-6) raises albedo and cuts evapotranspiration, drying further: a Sahel-style tipping loop coupling climate↔biosphere, the destabilizing counterpart to CLIM-ice-albedo's stabilizer | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-air-masses | The meso tier between the global bands and the per-cell field — continental/maritime/polar/tropical air masses and the frontal boundaries (storm tracks) where they collide, the natural home for CLIM-weather's synoptic storms and the organizing structure the flat classification skips | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-locked-regime | Enrich the tidally-locked model (`temperature.rs` gives it only a substellar cosine and a flat frozen night floor): add day→night atmospheric heat redistribution (which can prevent atmospheric collapse), the substellar convective cloud pillar, and the terminator habitable ring as first-class outputs — the eyeball-planet physics the M-dwarf worlds SKY-13 asks for will need | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-microclimate | The fine tier under "coarse constrains fine" — sub-cell refinement by slope aspect (pole- vs sun-facing), valley cold-air pooling and frost pockets, and coastal fog, refining biome and habitability locally without contradicting the coarse field: the constitutional refinement rule applied within the domain | raw | med | this conversation; ideonomy session |
| CLIM-felt-weather | The world is felt beyond the sky — `GeneratedClimate` becomes a `PhenomenaSource` emitting standing conditions (heat/cold/rain/snow, `Venue::Ambient`) that vary with (place×time), riding The Turning's diurnal and The Wandering Sun's seasonal terms. Salience = **deviation from a temperate baseline**: a narrow emission band means mild climes ARE felt (sub-floor), while a far deity floor means only a brutal clime (deep desert, ice waste) mints a weather-god — so weather-worship is *earned by hardship*, not sprinkled across every valley. Reached the stream via the **phenomena-source roster** (the `Domain` trait gains `phenomena_source`, retiring the six hardcoded fan-outs — the same roster seam as concept registration; adding a domain's phenomena now edits neither worldgen nor a sibling). Closes the [[PROC-16]] correspondence audit's rain/snow orphan-and-unperceived gaps from both sides at once. Lesson banked: the observation seam is a hot path — a provider must be cheap to *hold*, not just to *ask* (sources built once per generation) | shipped | — | [The Elements](../chronicle/the-elements.md) |
| CLIM-cloud-base | Occlusion has no **altitude**. The Occlusion made cloud the sky's second occluder (after daylight), but the deck is a property of the *cell*, not of a height: an observer on a peak above an overcast is dimmed exactly as much as one in the valley beneath it. Give the deck a drawn base altitude and compare it against the observer's elevation, and the mountain-top sky clears while the valley stays shut — the cheapest way to make elevation matter to the *sky* rather than only to temperature. Additive over the shipped `occlusion()` seam; no new physics, one comparison | raw | high (small) | [The Occlusion](../chronicle/the-occlusion.md) |
| CLIM-occluded-bodies | `SkyReport.bodies` is not occluded — the almanac still prints `Visible bodies: the sun, moon 1, moon 2` beneath a flat overcast, and names the sun at night. The field means "what is up there", which is defensible in isolation but reads as a leftover beside the now-occluded prose. Either run it through the same visibility the description uses, or rename the label to say what it means | raw | high (small) | [The Occlusion](../chronicle/the-occlusion.md) |
| LOC-exotic-commensurability | The placed-exotic tier shipped **one outcome**: 92-98% of every world's sites were fungal. **Shipped in The Tare**, and it was two defects. The fungal score's own comment promised "damp, low-relief, non-volcanic" and computed only non-volcanic — `1.0 - unrest`, a near-constant default that beat the vent candidates' raw `unrest` almost everywhere; `drainage` supplies the missing damp clause. And `Kingdom::Crystalline` was defined with authored prose but in NO candidate, so it could not occur at all. Fungal 92-98% -> 61-73%; crystalline 0 -> 20-32 sites per world | shipped | high | [The Tare](../chronicle/the-tare.md) |
| LOC-marine-prose | Roughly **70% of the planet had no prose**: twelve of twenty-two biomes fell through a two-item catch-all, and `micro_habitat` applied land clauses on top, so a black smoker 2500m down read "sun-warmed" and a kelp forest read "dry". **Shipped in The Shoal:** ten marine formations plus Ice and Shrubland authored, and the water column now reads the micro-field in its own terms — relief is the floor beneath, aspect is light only where light arrives, wetness becomes the current. The structural payoff of the facets is the open-water arm, one formation read by its depth | shipped | high | [The Shoal](../chronicle/the-shoal.md) |
| LOC-narrator-stance | The room description asserted a posture nothing had computed — "You stand in" from a template that knew the medium and the band and nothing about legs, fins, wings, boats or sleep. **Resolved in The Effacement:** the narrator withdrew; the place opens the sentence and the body belongs to whoever owns it, which is the convention tabletop read-aloud text reached for the same reason. Command responses still address the player, since the command is their source. A SOURCED stance is still open and wants the liveness layer to expose a real activity | shipped | high | [The Effacement](../chronicle/the-effacement.md) |
| LOC-water-column | Can a ship pass above while sea elves dwell below? **Shipped in The Column:** a depth band mirroring The Lintel's `inside` — `dive` and `surface` move between the strata of a marine coordinate, the column's depth set by the sea floor, and the formation read as open water everywhere above the floor because a community lives on the bottom. The stance follows the medium: you float on, hang in, or stand in. Swimming is deliberately absent — lateral movement while submerged is refused, and traversal wants the destination's floor depth first | shipped | high | [The Column](../chronicle/the-column.md) |
| LOC-strangeness-gradient | Strange sites are findable from outside (`locale --strange`) but not from *inside* the world. Surface strangeness on possession's existing `map` verb so a walker gets a gradient to follow rather than an answer handed over — a path, not a spoiler table. Scoped into The Occlusion's plan and dropped under its own escape hatch once `map` proved a substantial chart renderer with its own zoom and bounds handling, rather than the wiring job it looked like | raw | med | [The Occlusion](../chronicle/the-occlusion.md) |
| LOC-biome-facets | The biome taxonomy as `realm : formation : stratum : variant : overlay` rather than one flat enum — `Savanna` and `Bathypelagic` are not siblings, and the classifier's precedence chain (returning a *community* where a *stratum* was expected) was the symptom. **Campaign 1 shipped in The Formations:** `Biome` survives as the *projection* of a `BiomeExpr`, so nine consuming crates were untouched and no world byte moved; realm is a triple, so a sky realm or a plane is a new value, not a new axis. Remaining: variants and their gloss epoch, exotic commensurability, the depth band | shipped | high | [The Stratum spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-the-stratum-design.md); [The Formations](../chronicle/the-formations.md) |

| ID    | Idea                                                                                                                                                                                         | Status     | Conf | Where                                                                                              |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------- | ---- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SEQ-1 | Tidal lock is the cheapest alien-religion generator — a motionless sun forces a religion with no celestial cycles                                                                            | elaborated | high | [frontier §year-1 sequencing](frontier.md#two-sequencing-notes-for-the-remaining-year-1-campaigns) |
| SEQ-2 | Hydrology is people-infrastructure, not land-refinement — rivers are the skeleton of settlement, trade, borders, toponymy. **The settlement-siting slice shipped** (campaign *The Confluence*): the carrying-capacity field's freshwater term is re-pointed at real proximity to river cells (built on *The Freshet*'s salt/fresh `WaterKind` classification), so settlements condense onto/adjacent-to the real river network, emergently — measured, not authored (seed 42: a preregistered near-river fraction of 0.72, up from a pre-repoint 0.58). The wider infrastructure layer this row names (named rivers as entities, trade routes, borders, crossings/fords, bridges) is still unbuilt | shipped (settlement-siting slice) | high | [frontier §year-1 sequencing](frontier.md#two-sequencing-notes-for-the-remaining-year-1-campaigns); [The Freshet chronicle](../chronicle/the-freshet.md); [The Confluence chronicle](../chronicle/the-confluence.md) |
| SEQ-3 | The deep-time stack DAG — 7 → 9 → {language demographics, genetic divergence}; racecraft downstream of subjugation; coevolution last                                                         | elaborated | —    | [frontier §deep-time stack](frontier.md#sequencing-the-deep-time-stack)                            |
| SEQ-4 | Phenomena-path fidelity — generated worlds observe through tier-0 UniformClimate, anchored at places[0] and genesis day 0; a future Firm-Ground-style campaign upgrades the observation path | shipped        | high | [Firm Ground II spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-firm-ground-ii-design.md)         |
| SEQ-5 | The observer has no place — phenomena are delivered position-blind. `ObserverContext.place` is an `EntityId` with no location on the globe, so the sky can't be culled by horizon: on a tidally-locked world the fixed sun reaches its own night side and the neighbour stars reach the permanent day side, and on a spinning world the moons/stars gate on the *global* `is_daylight` clock, not the observer's longitude. The spatial sibling of SEQ-4 and of EXP-3's *perceptual* observer — the fix extends `ObserverContext` (not `Phenomenon`; position is the observer's, not the body's), the seam MAP-2's "one hemisphere of sky" almanac assumes | shipped        | high | [Firm Ground II spec](https://github.com/hornvale/hornvale/blob/main/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-09-firm-ground-ii-design.md)   |
